Dear lawmakers, first of all, I'd like to address the problem caused by a very wrong political attitude that disrupts the functioning of minority foundations and suspends even basic democratic principles. See, for five years, minority foundations have had a problem of election regulations suspended for five years, and this problem must be resolved immediately. Unfortunately, since January 2013, our community foundation has been deprived of the right to choose and choose, the most basic principle of organization and governance. There has been no improvement in regulations so far, and freedom of choice and selection is a very grave situation in preventing the freedom of organisation. Even though the mandates of the permanent election have expired, the use of the powers of the next administrations prevents our foundations from being led freely, democratically and transparently. This is to put a mortgage on the true will of members of the congregation's foundations. It is the duty of the General Directorate of Foundations to ensure justice, equality, lawlessness and legal loopholes are cleared. I'd like to address Mr. Hakan Sergeantoglu who is in charge of the foundations and our General Manager of the Foundations once again. First of all, I'd like to continue by bringing this question up and reminding it to be resolved immediately again in front of Parliament. Dear lawmakers, the main issues facing a solution to minority foundations are the issue of various forms of impounded immovable property. The issue of confiscation of residences belonging to minority foundations regarding our non-Muslim citizens is one of the major problems facing Turkey that has been pending a solution for a long time. In the Republic of Turkey, most of the institutions that are central to the religious, social, and cultural survival of minorities are community foundations established during the Ottoman period. After the Act of the Foundations took effect, in 1936 all foundations were obliged to declare and register their property. After 1936, minority foundations were able to acquire real estate either by buying or by donating, and they were able to register them on the deed. However, by the Supreme Court's decision of 1974, these declarations began to be accepted as a foundation, and after that, hundreds of moving sites belonging to the Keldani and the Jewish community have been seized as a result of lawsuits that led to this decision by the Supreme Court in succession. Mr. President, good lawmakers, some positive legal regulations have been carried out during the Justice and Development Party governments, 2003, 2008 and 2011, as well as the cases of minority foundations in the European Court of Human Rights over the course of time. However, these regulations did not contain minority-owned foundations, nor did they provide inclusive solutions to the matter of confiscated transports. Dear lawmakers, the current form and application of the interim Article 11 of the Act of the Foundations and its implementation are largely inadequate to address some of the problems of minority foundations. In the interim article, the condition that the restitution to minority foundations is registered in the 1936 declaration leads to significant property rights violations and sacrifices. The basis of the 1936 declaration of this clause deprives non-1936 foundations of property rights. On the other hand, for example, it is not possible for Hatay to have a 1936 declaration because Hatay was not a member of Turkey in 1936. Another important flaw in the 11th article is that it is registered in the 1936 declaration and does not contain a regulation on the transports that are owned by parish foundations but are registered to public institutions or third parties. Mr. President, valued lawmakers, in this context, I'd like to address the problems of the Süryani people in the Mardin region that have recently been registered to the Treasury on various occasions. Before 2008, cadastro studies in some villages in Mardin and Midyat were registered for the legal and judicial decisions of the foundation, since it was not possible to move property in the name of churches, monasteries, graveyards and villages in which they were not moving. Later, when the village's corporate personalities were eliminated along with the Metropolitan Law, some of these real estates were registered to the municipality where they were located, and some were transferred to the General Directorate of the Foundations. This has led to significant victims of the Süryanian foundations in the region. Of course, then Deputy Prime Minister Hakan Sergeant, in charge of the foundations, stated that they would extradite the property, saying that there was a misdemeanor transaction at the desk. The Minister's statements have produced satisfaction in the Suryanian community, but no concrete arrangement has been made yet. We'll be waiting for the arrangements on this matter as soon as possible. Another issue: As you may recall, the court proceedings for the 18 parts of the 12-part foundation of the 30-part move to the Purple Gabriel Foundation, which was confiscated under the President's democraticisation package in 2013, continue. On this occasion, we repeat our call at the point of immediate resolving this problem. Mr. President, good lawmakers, as a result, the request of our citizens regarding minority foundations is that, regardless of whether they are registered in the 1936 Statement, they should be extradited to the relevant foundations, especially in the Armenian, Greek, Süryani, Jews, Keldani, Maruni, Georgian and Bulgarian foundations, taken from the hands of the foundations taken into the court, and sold to third parties without any condition. We, too, speak before Parliament once again about this request of our citizens. Mr. President, valued lawmakers, of course, it's not possible to address the problems of minority foundations here in a ten-minute period. But I also want to stress that restrictions on religious education, an important component of freedom of religion and faith, continue to be one of the main problems facing minorities. In this regard, the fact that the Heybeliada seminary has not yet been opened against Lausanne remains a flaw in our country, and it continues to offend our Christian citizens in this sense. Mr. President, considering the Lausanne Treaty, the European Convention on Human Rights and other international contracts that Turkey is a party to, the immediate fulfillment of legal regulations that will eliminate existing, democratic and universal lawless practices will ensure that the problems in question will be dealt with. It is imperative that these problems be considered a matter of democracy, human rights and the state of law and considered within the framework of concepts such as equal citizenship, constitutional citizenship. This budget is not a rightful budget, but rather an unfair and unconscionable budget. Dear lawmakers, I have made a pledge to protect personal data. In many texts where the concept of personal data is accepted in the world, it is expressed as all real information of a specific and defined person. Personal data are under the absolute protection of the rule of privacy of private life in Article 20 of the Constitution, and of Article 8 of the European Convention on Human Rights, as well as of Article 17 of the United Nations Convention on Civil Rights and Political Rights. The person who owns personal data is the real person. People have to decide what's shared and what's confidential. The Protection of Personal Data Law, which took effect last year, included positive provisions for the preservation of personal data, but exceptions to the intelligence and security devices of the state, which have the real data monopoly, which have practically all citizens. According to this law, the activities of institutions such as the Carabinieri, MIT, MASAK, and the Police are an exception. Look, dear Parliament members, the 28th Article of the Law (1) has a decree that personal data will be covered in full exception if it is carried out under the law and within the scope of preventive, protective and employment activities carried out by authorized public institutions and institutions aimed at providing national defence, national security, public safety, public order or economic security. On the basis of this joke, it's almost legalized that all citizens be branded. Dear MPs, there are two basic dimensions to the establishment of the institution and the removal of Law 6698. The first is economic size. As you know, the desire to make more money is historical. In particular, in our digital age, the data economy has a very important property. The data of individuals has now become even more valuable than oil. In order to make more profit than the presentation of goods and services, private sectors were storing data of permanent individuals against their will, which is still going on. As a result of this data hoarding of capital, the most valuable and private data of individuals is being thrown around. As a result, private lives are being deciphered, and fundamental rights and freedoms are also violated. This institution and the data economy have gone into a Regulation to ensure that it can be maintained in a certain order. While the private sector is now processing personal data, it has to take open consent from people and fulfill its obligation to enlighten them. In order for real people to give capital data freely, a trust relationship had to be established because now people are afraid to share their data. The lawmakers basically established this institution so that the capital can see its work better, and the data economy can sustain it. Dear lawmakers, the second basic issue of the establishment of this institution is the political dimension. For years, the European Union progress reports have demanded that the Protection of Personal Data Law be established based on this law and that an institution be formed. The law, which has the largest data monopoly in the rundown negotiations, has all of the security devices of the state as exceptions, and like capital's greed for profit, the state wants to imprison permanent citizens in a control circle. The government uses personal data to achieve its political goals or to protect its own political interests. It is often impossible to mention that sufficient protection has been provided for individual personal information and data while the state's information, the secret of state, is closely guarded by concepts that are difficult to establish boundaries such as national security. The state, which often holds individual data or demands to present this information in public service, is in the process of processing, storing individual data, sharing it with non-relevant individuals or institutions, putting the individual in a passive position on its own data. Information in health, insurance, banking, education, and other areas that can help capital make more money in particular is becoming more valuable, personal data is being converted into an unsub that is being bought and sold. Independent institutions, which are not being instructed from anywhere against the profit ambition of capital and the desire to impose unlimited power and the desire to impose citizens, can at least bring about a little bit of a balance in the civil-employment and civic-government relationship. So, is the Personal Data Protection Agency established within the framework of this law an independent structure? I'm afraid not. Because members are based on the majority structure of power. Five members of the ÜMM are determined by the election; the presidency and the Council of Ministers are appointing each other; two members of the Council of Ministers with the amendment will now be appointed by the President, who is also the President of the AKP. In addition, the 19th article of the law (2) is referred to as the Prime Minister of the Court. In Turkey's administration law, unfortunately, this relationship is becoming a relationship dependent on what is called a verb. In addition, the exceptions to the law have been decided that the state can process all of our private information without question. Protecting personal data should carry the purpose of protecting the individual's data against all individuals and institutions, especially the state with the largest data monopoly. In this regard, it weakens the Data Protection Act without strengthening the Data Protection Act, with the exception of data protection legislation of almost all institutions and actions, in terms of both different areas and actions. I think that human, citizen's superior interests should be our basic criteria rather than the state's. All approaches, which are blessed by the state, that are pushed into the second plan, are not solutions, but problems. Thus, we need to approach it within the framework of the humanization of security, a concept that has often been discussed in recent years. Dear friends, in order for the Institute for Protection of Personal Data to be independent, as the law states, the institution must be truly administratively and financially autonomous and must be based on the principle of rights in the distribution of board members according to parties. I'm finishing my vows by saying that there must be a strong co-ordination between civil society organisations and the institution that have been fighting for years in the field of fundamental rights and freedoms, and that the institution must have a budget to create its own resources so that it can be independent, not dependent. I salute the General Assembly.

tbmm-2017-12-13sit03spe0232par0303	Mr. President, I have made a pledge on behalf of our AK PARTY Group on the budget of 2018 of the Banking Organization and Control. The BDDK is an autonomous public institution established in the 1990s to free its banking sector from fluctuations and the fragmented structure of control institutions. The banking sector in Turkey went through a pretty rough period in the 1990s. The main reason for this crisis is that the banks were heavily invested in public property due to insufficient control, and they worked in high foreign currency open positions. This tendency to take extreme risks has led to the economic crisis of 1994. Turkish lira has been overvalued, heavy economic costs have emerged. At this time, 11 banks were seized by 1999 due to structural problems in the banking sector. In June 1999, BDK was established with the Council of Ministers Decision 4389 in order to increase auditing and regulation practices and to establish independent decision-making mechanisms, and began its activities in September 2000. The duties of the BDDK are under Banking Law 5411. The BDDK supervises banks on the basis of their affidavits, IT experts and legal experts, particularly the control of banks and risk-oriented control approach. In the period before the establishment of the BDK (BDK) and in the process of the establishment of the BDK (BDK), from 1994 to 2001, 20 banks were transferred to TMSF as a result of financial crises, while five banks were cancelled. This has cost our economy over $50 billion. Mr. President, the valuable lawmakers are continuing to have a positive impact on our country's economic development with both active quality and profitability performance, including the banking sector, which holds 86 per cent of its share within the financial system. As of October 2017, the Turkish banking sector, including 52 banks, has reached an active size of 3.2 trillion euros. The amount of deposits, which are of the most important sources of our banking system, is 1.7 trillion TL, which has contributed substantially to the growth of the country through these funds, which have been channeled to loans and other assets, through the size of $349 billion of equity, the reputation and the variety of funds it has established over the years. As of October 2017, we have been closely monitoring the effects of incentives for SMEs in the banking sector, which has 2 trillion euros in credit volumes, from our economy's growth and employment dynamics. As it is known, the Council of Ministers' decision of March 10, 2017, aimed at facilitating the access to the financing of the SMEs, provided by the Treasury Undersecretary to increase the source from 2 billion TL to 25 billion TL and to increase the bail volume from 20 billion TL to 250 billion TL, has had significant impacts on both the banking sector and growth. The proportion of SME loans in GDP has reached 29 per cent of the 15 per cent. The remaining private area, where human rights is most violated, can only be entered if open consent is found.
